The
bigon

\mathbb{B}
is a disc with two boundary edges. The
punctured bigon

\mathbb{D}_{1}
is an annulus with two boundary edges in the same boundary component.
\mathbf{\Sigma}(g,n)
is the surface
\Sigma_{g,n+1}
with one boundary edge. The
punctured monogon

\mathbf{m}_{1}=(\Sigma_{0,2},\{b\})
is an annulus with a single boundary edge.

Algebras

\bullet
An
almost Azumaya algebra

\mathscr{A}
is a complex algebra which is prime, affine, of finitely generated as a module over its center
Z
. Its
PI-degree

D
is defined by
D^{2}=\dim_{Q(Z)}\mathscr{A}\otimes_{Z}Q(Z)
. Its
Azumaya locus
is

\mathcal{AL}(\mathscr{A}):=\{x\in\operatorname{MaxSpec}(Z):{\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\mathscr{A}$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$x\mathscr{A}$}\right.}\cong\operatorname{Mat}_{D}(\mathbb{C})\}.

\bullet

A^{1/2}\in\mathbb{C}
is a root of unity of odd order
N\geq 3
.
\mathcal{S}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
is the
stated skein algebra
of
\mathbf{\Sigma}
.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
is the
reduced stated skein algebra
of
\mathbf{\Sigma}
. We set
\mathcal{L}_{g,n}:=\mathcal{S}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma}(g,n))
.

We consider
Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\subset Z^{1}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\subset Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{S}_{A}}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
where
Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
is the center of
\overline{\mathcal{S}_{A}}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
,
Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
is the image of the Frobenius and
Z^{1}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
is the central subalgebra generated by
Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
and the boundary central elements
\alpha_{\partial}^{\pm 1}
(see Definition
2.24
).

\bullet
We write

X(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\operatorname{MaxSpec}(Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}),\quad\widehat{X}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\operatorname{MaxSpec}(Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}})\quad\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\{\mathfrak{m}\in\operatorname{Spec}(Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}):\mathfrak{m}\cap Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{0}\in X(\mathbf{\Sigma})\}

and denote by
\pi:\widehat{X}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\to X(\mathbf{\Sigma})
and
\widehat{\pi}:\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\to X(\mathbf{\Sigma})
the natural projections sending
\mathfrak{m}\subset Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
to
\mathfrak{m}\cap Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\subset Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
.

Set
\mathcal{AL}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\mathcal{AL}(\overline{\mathcal{S}_{A}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}))
. The
fully Azumaya locus
is

\mathcal{FAL}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\{x\in X(\mathbf{\Sigma}):\pi^{-1}(x)\subset\mathcal{AL}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\}.

\bullet
Similarly we set
\underline{Z}^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\subset\underline{Z}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\subset\mathcal{S}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
the image of the Frobenius and the center respectively and set

\underline{X}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\operatorname{MaxSpec}(\underline{Z}^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}),\quad\underline{\widehat{X}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\operatorname{MaxSpec}(\underline{Z}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}).

Representations

\bullet
A representation
r:\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\to\operatorname{End}(V)
is a
weight representation
if it is semi-simple as module over
Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
and is a
semi-weight representation
if it is semi-simple as a module over
Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
. A representation
r:\mathcal{S}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\to\operatorname{End}(V)
is
reduced
if it factorizes as
r:\mathcal{S}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\xrightarrow{j}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{*})\to\operatorname{End}(V)
.

\bullet
Let
r:\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{A}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\to\operatorname{End}(V)
be an indecomposable semi-weight representation.

(1)

Its
classical shadow
is the maximal ideal
x_{r}\in X(\mathbf{\Sigma})
defined by
x_{r}=\ker(r)\cap Z^{0}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
.

(2)

Its
full shadow
is the prime ideal
\mathcal{I}_{r}\in\widehat{\mathcal{X}}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
defined by
\mathcal{I}_{r}:=\ker(r)\cap Z_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
.

(3)

Its
maximal shadow
is the unique maximal ideal
\widehat{x}_{r}\in\widehat{X}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
which contains
\mathcal{I}_{r}
.

In particular
{\pi}(\widehat{x}_{r})=\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{I}_{r})=x_{r}
and
\mathcal{I}_{r}=\widehat{x}_{r}\in\widehat{X}(\mathbf{\Sigma})
if and only if
r
is weight representation.

Moduli spaces

\bullet
When
\mathbf{\Sigma}
is essential, the
representation variety
is

\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):=\operatorname{Hom}(\pi_{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{V}),\operatorname{SL}_{2}).

The
reduced representation variety
is

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):\{\rho\in\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}):\rho(\alpha_{\partial})\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{1},\forall\partial\in\Gamma^{\partial}\}.

Here we consider the simple Bruhat decomposition
\operatorname{SL}_{2}=\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{0}\sqcup\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{1}
where

\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{0}:=\left\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}:a\neq 0\right\}.
